# Unit 22 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 22 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 22 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

title=One agrees with its noun
body=один мальчик — one boy. одна девушка — one girl. The numeral meaning one agrees with its noun in gender, number and case. These displayed counting phrases use a singular noun: the masculine boy takes the masculine numeral, and the feminine girl takes the feminine numeral. The guided choice asks for another phrase with one, not the same noun.

kind=TEACH
title=The scope of the counting rule
body=For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.

kind=TEACH
title=Two, three and four form a group
body=две девушки — two girls. три кошки — three cats. Both displayed nouns are genitive singular after these numerals, although the quantity is greater than one and these endings can resemble nominative plural. The numeral for two has a feminine form; the form for three does not change for the noun gender. The check asks for another example in the two-through-four group, not an English translation of the cue.

kind=TEACH
title=Five and higher in the displayed group
body=пять девушек — five girls. десять копеек — ten kopeks. Here the nouns are genitive plural. Five and ten belong to the same noun-form group; one and two do not. Numbers whose last two digits are 11 through 14 also use this group, including 111 through 114. These choices recognize supplied number phrases; they do not test independently producing every noun ending.

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete number contrast
body=один мальчик — one boy. одна девушка — one girl. The numeral meaning one agrees with its noun in gender, number and case. These displayed counting phrases use a singular noun: the masculine boy takes the masculine numeral, and the feminine girl takes the feminine numeral. The guided choice asks for another phrase with one, not the same noun.

For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.

две девушки — two girls. три кошки — three cats. Both displayed nouns are genitive singular after these numerals, although the quantity is greater than one and these endings can resemble nominative plural. The numeral for two has a feminine form; the form for three does not change for the noun gender. The check asks for another example in the two-through-four group, not an English translation of the cue.

пять девушек — five girls. десять копеек — ten kopeks. Here the nouns are genitive plural. Five and ten belong to the same noun-form group; one and two do not. Numbers whose last two digits are 11 through 14 also use this group, including 111 through 114. These choices recognize supplied number phrases; they do not test independently producing every noun ending.

title=Three endings in compound quantities
body=тридцать три рюмки — thirty-three shot glasses. сорок один год — forty-one years. In these nominative counting phrases, 33 ends in three and takes a genitive singular noun; 41 ends in one and takes a singular noun. The choice may ask for a different final-digit group from the displayed reference phrase. Read the requested group in the question.

- Content hash: 6bbc916260054b4e9c6fe43c2efc3926a93f5150adcba0fb94c996b5837a87f5

kind=TEACH
title=Check the last two digits first
body=For these whole-number counting phrases, check the last two digits before the final digit. Endings 11 through 14 require genitive plural. Otherwise a final one takes singular, final two through four take genitive singular, and final zero or five through nine take genitive plural. For example, 111 differs from 41; 113 differs from 33. These are numeric rule comparisons, not newly supplied Russian phrases. In other cases the numeral and noun need the relevant declined forms.

kind=TEACH
title=Nine and the requested category
body=шестьдесят девять слов — sixty-nine words. The final nine places 69 in the genitive-plural group. In the three choices, сорок один год ends in one, тридцать три рюмки ends in three, and шестьдесят девять слов ends in nine. Identify the category requested by the question. A displayed 41 phrase does not make the answer to a question about nine a translation or a same-quantity counterpart.

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete number contrast
body=тридцать три рюмки — thirty-three shot glasses. сорок один год — forty-one years. In these nominative counting phrases, 33 ends in three and takes a genitive singular noun; 41 ends in one and takes a singular noun. The choice may ask for a different final-digit group from the displayed reference phrase. Read the requested group in the question.

For these whole-number counting phrases, check the last two digits before the final digit. Endings 11 through 14 require genitive plural. Otherwise a final one takes singular, final two through four take genitive singular, and final zero or five through nine take genitive plural. For example, 111 differs from 41; 113 differs from 33. These are numeric rule comparisons, not newly supplied Russian phrases. In other cases the numeral and noun need the relevant declined forms.

шестьдесят девять слов — sixty-nine words. The final nine places 69 in the genitive-plural group. In the three choices, сорок один год ends in one, тридцать три рюмки ends in three, and шестьдесят девять слов ends in nine. Identify the category requested by the question. A displayed 41 phrase does not make the answer to a question about nine a translation or a same-quantity counterpart.

kind=TEACH
title=A changed form can name a different case
body=двум — Дательный, the dative. двумя — Творительный, the instrumental. Both are declined forms of the numeral meaning two. Dative commonly marks a recipient or goal; instrumental can mark accompaniment or means. The final part of the form distinguishes these two shown examples; it is not enough to recognize the quantity two.

kind=TEACH
title=Read a row in its case context
body=Дательный means dative; Творительный means instrumental; Предложный means prepositional. A form must be interpreted with its construction. The bare form двух can occur in more than one case. The admitted row includes the preposition and is labelled prepositional; the check does not claim that a bare ending always uniquely identifies a case. In oblique number phrases the plural noun normally carries the case required by the construction.

title=The displayed prepositional row
body=(о) двух / (об) обоих / (о) полутора — Предложный. This complete row gives the prepositional forms for two, both and one and a half. The parentheses show the accompanying preposition; it is not part of the numeral itself. The prepositional choice contrasts with the dative двум and instrumental двумя. Recognizing this row does not demonstrate independently declining a complete number phrase.

kind=TEACH
title=Dative both has two gender forms
body=обеим — Дательный. обоим — Дательный. Both forms mean both in the dative. The feminine series has the stem seen in обеим; the masculine/neuter series has the stem seen in обоим. Gender refers to the counted noun. Changing the gender series does not change the case: both displayed forms are dative.

kind=TEACH
title=One and a half changes for gender
body=полтора́ рубля́ — 1.5 roubles. полторы́ мину́ты — 1.5 minutes. The first numeral form is masculine/neuter; the second is feminine. In the nominative and accusative construction, one and a half takes a genitive singular noun. The noun determines the numeral gender even though the amount exceeds one. The retained alternative gloss one and a half hours / an hour and a half names hours, not roubles or minutes.

title=From, to and with have different constructions
body=из полу́тора рубле́й — from 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires genitive plural on the noun. к полу́тора рубля́м — to 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires dative plural. The English distractor with 1.5 roubles names accompaniment/means with an instrumental noun in the corresponding retained source construction. The amount stays one and a half while the preposition changes the relationship. The check asks for the meaning of the whole shown phrase, including from or to.

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete number contrast
body=полтора́ рубля́ — 1.5 roubles. полторы́ мину́ты — 1.5 minutes. The first numeral form is masculine/neuter; the second is feminine. In the nominative and accusative construction, one and a half takes a genitive singular noun. The noun determines the numeral gender even though the amount exceeds one. The retained alternative gloss one and a half hours / an hour and a half names hours, not roubles or minutes.

In the other cases, the numeral has the shared form полутора, and the noun is plural in the case required by the construction. The numeral form alone therefore cannot tell you whether a phrase is genitive, dative or instrumental. Compare the preposition and the noun ending. This differs from the genitive singular noun after the nominative/accusative forms.

из полу́тора рубле́й — from 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires genitive plural on the noun. к полу́тора рубля́м — to 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires dative plural. The English distractor with 1.5 roubles names accompaniment/means with an instrumental noun in the corresponding retained source construction. The amount stays one and a half while the preposition changes the relationship. The check asks for the meaning of the whole shown phrase, including from or to.

title=Distinguish cardinal, ordinal and collective number forms

### STEP `u22-st-teach-collective-1`

- Content hash: e35e8c863b60d98479c3e54aa023f356660ab2064a2debbd3c397a2ae5ea353a

kind=TEACH
title=Two and three as collective quantities
body=два — дво́е. три — тро́е. A cardinal states a quantity; a collective presents that quantity as a group. An ordinal states position in a sequence. Thus дво́е is the collective for two, and тро́е is the collective for three. The option второ́й means second, and тре́тий means third; these are ordinal positions, not collective quantities.
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u2125-attribution
items=
examples=ex-u22-collective-0,ex-u22-collective-1
unavailableReason=
bookDirection=

kind=TEACH
title=Collectives have restricted uses
body=Collective number forms are not a general replacement for cardinal numbers with every noun. Standard uses include groups of male people, children or people expressed by appropriate group nouns, personal pronouns, and certain nouns used only in the plural. Use ordinary cardinals with female-person nouns in standard usage; do not infer that any feminine noun takes a collective. This lesson explains the restriction without introducing new Russian noun combinations.

kind=TEACH
title=Four and the limits of these checks
body=четы́ре — че́тверо. The collective form keeps the quantity four while presenting it as a group. The ordinal distractor четвёртый means fourth, a position; дво́е is a group of two. In the earlier check, че́тверо is a collective but has the wrong quantity for three. Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.

### STEP `u22-st-rem-collective`

- Objectives: obj-u22-collective
- Sessions: u22-s-collective
- Source: none
- Rights: none
- Classification: none
- Current review status: NO_SEPARATE_REVIEW_FIELD
- Content hash: a289960325c4d2d4acd8d0a3e157e50298fb3058e18316db03266e8ed418ae4c

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete number contrast
body=два — дво́е. три — тро́е. A cardinal states a quantity; a collective presents that quantity as a group. An ordinal states position in a sequence. Thus дво́е is the collective for two, and тро́е is the collective for three. The option второ́й means second, and тре́тий means third; these are ordinal positions, not collective quantities.

Collective number forms are not a general replacement for cardinal numbers with every noun. Standard uses include groups of male people, children or people expressed by appropriate group nouns, personal pronouns, and certain nouns used only in the plural. Use ordinary cardinals with female-person nouns in standard usage; do not infer that any feminine noun takes a collective. This lesson explains the restriction without introducing new Russian noun combinations.

четы́ре — че́тверо. The collective form keeps the quantity four while presenting it as a group. The ordinal distractor четвёртый means fourth, a position; дво́е is a group of two. In the earlier check, че́тверо is a collective but has the wrong quantity for three. Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.
dominantAction=Continue

English Wikipedia Russian grammar oldid 1369617627 is used under CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikipedia:Copyrights oldid 1369466282. Russian Wikipedia pages are used under CC BY-SA 4.0 via the Russian Wikipedia copyrights page oldid 154562152. The one-and-a-half entry is frozen at English Wiktionary oldid 89737719 under CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wiktionary:Copyrights oldid 87545419. Linked audio is not admitted. Unit 23 time expressions are frozen Pressbooks chapter bytes sha256 87815b8724cb11aecba5bb6d8b6438f783408f2354e2560a3c0fe026fac1e171, retrieved 2026-09-26T22:52:41Z, under that chapter's own CC BY-SA 4.0 footer. Russian Wikipedia personal-name page oldid 154915954 is retained for bounded classification of the documented full-form and short-form diminutive bases, the dismissive-suffix limitation, and the lowered-status note, under the Russian Wikipedia copyrights page oldid 154562152. A separate application chain on that same span uses only names already in the span. It is not a name generator. The Unit 24 word-order pair is bounded sentence recognition on English Wikipedia Russian grammar oldid 1369617627. It does not clear scope-sensitive placement. Scope-sensitive placement is the ReadyRussian Language Generator 3 statement that the modifier negates the following word, and that page's two-sentence contrast, under the ReadyRussian CC BY-SA 4.0 route. recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-both-forms:both-forms-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
обеим — Дательный. обоим — Дательный. Both forms mean both in the dative. The feminine series has the stem seen in обеим; the masculine/neuter series has the stem seen in обоим. Gender refers to the counted noun. Changing the gender series does not change the case: both displayed forms are dative.

Both refers to the full pair, rather than an unspecified quantity of two. The retained nominative table distinguishes masculine/neuter and feminine forms. The dative row двум / обеим / полутора places two, feminine both and one and a half together by case, not by equal meaning. Дательный is dative, Творительный is instrumental, and Предложный is prepositional.

обеими — Творительный. обоими — Творительный. The feminine and masculine/neuter series remain distinct, but both shown forms are instrumental. Compare обеим with обеими and обоим with обоими: the extra final vowel distinguishes these dative and instrumental examples. The current choices check the case of a supplied form; they do not test producing the correct gender-and-case form in a new sentence.

Gender refers to the counted noun. Changing the gender series does not change the case: both displayed forms are dative.

Both refers to the full pair, rather than an unspecified quantity of two. The retained nominative table distinguishes masculine/neuter and feminine forms. The dative row двум / обеим / полутора places two, feminine both and one and a half together by case, not by equal meaning. Дательный is dative, Творительный is instrumental, and Предложный is prepositional.

обеими — Творительный. обоими — Творительный. The feminine and masculine/neuter series remain distinct, but both shown forms are instrumental. Compare обеим with обеими and обоим with обоими: the extra final vowel distinguishes these dative and instrumental examples. The current choices check the case of a supplied form; they do not test producing the correct gender-and-case form in a new sentence.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-both-forms:both-forms-teach-2-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
Both refers to the full pair, rather than an unspecified quantity of two. The retained nominative table distinguishes masculine/neuter and feminine forms. The dative row двум / обеим / полутора places two, feminine both and one and a half together by case, not by equal meaning. Дательный is dative, Творительный is instrumental, and Предложный is prepositional.

обеими — Творительный. обоими — Творительный. The feminine and masculine/neuter series remain distinct, but both shown forms are instrumental. Compare обеим with обеими and обоим with обоими: the extra final vowel distinguishes these dative and instrumental examples. The current choices check the case of a supplied form; they do not test producing the correct gender-and-case form in a new sentence.

### PROVENANCE `prov-ca-u22-collective-rem-body`

- Objectives: obj-u22-collective
- Sessions: u22-s-orient, u22-s-collective
- Source: src-content-audit-research
- Rights: rights-content-audit-research
- Classification: none
- Current review status: IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT
- Content hash: d4354aae4060bebf0684acc3794850943e6a58dbd7bd4922f9123047913c7691

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-collective:collective-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
два — дво́е. три — тро́е. A cardinal states a quantity; a collective presents that quantity as a group. An ordinal states position in a sequence. Thus дво́е is the collective for two, and тро́е is the collective for three. The option второ́й means second, and тре́тий means third; these are ordinal positions, not collective quantities.

Collective number forms are not a general replacement for cardinal numbers with every noun. Standard uses include groups of male people, children or people expressed by appropriate group nouns, personal pronouns, and certain nouns used only in the plural. Use ordinary cardinals with female-person nouns in standard usage; do not infer that any feminine noun takes a collective. This lesson explains the restriction without introducing new Russian noun combinations.

четы́ре — че́тверо. The collective form keeps the quantity four while presenting it as a group. The ordinal distractor четвёртый means fourth, a position; дво́е is a group of two. In the earlier check, че́тверо is a collective but has the wrong quantity for three. Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.
shippedText=два — дво́е. три — тро́е. A cardinal states a quantity; a collective presents that quantity as a group. An ordinal states position in a sequence. Thus дво́е is the collective for two, and тро́е is the collective for three. The option второ́й means second, and тре́тий means third; these are ordinal positions, not collective quantities.

Collective number forms are not a general replacement for cardinal numbers with every noun. Standard uses include groups of male people, children or people expressed by appropriate group nouns, personal pronouns, and certain nouns used only in the plural. Use ordinary cardinals with female-person nouns in standard usage; do not infer that any feminine noun takes a collective. This lesson explains the restriction without introducing new Russian noun combinations.

In the earlier check, че́тверо is a collective but has the wrong quantity for three. Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.

четы́ре — че́тверо. The collective form keeps the quantity four while presenting it as a group. The ordinal distractor четвёртый means fourth, a position; дво́е is a group of two. In the earlier check, че́тверо is a collective but has the wrong quantity for three. Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.

Selecting the collective column does not demonstrate choosing its use with every noun or constructing a declined collective phrase.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-compounds:compounds-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
тридцать три рюмки — thirty-three shot glasses. сорок один год — forty-one years. In these nominative counting phrases, 33 ends in three and takes a genitive singular noun; 41 ends in one and takes a singular noun. The choice may ask for a different final-digit group from the displayed reference phrase. Read the requested group in the question.

For these whole-number counting phrases, check the last two digits before the final digit. Endings 11 through 14 require genitive plural. Otherwise a final one takes singular, final two through four take genitive singular, and final zero or five through nine take genitive plural. For example, 111 differs from 41; 113 differs from 33. These are numeric rule comparisons, not newly supplied Russian phrases. In other cases the numeral and noun need the relevant declined forms.

шестьдесят девять слов — sixty-nine words. The final nine places 69 in the genitive-plural group. In the three choices, сорок один год ends in one, тридцать три рюмки ends in three, and шестьдесят девять слов ends in nine. Identify the category requested by the question. A displayed 41 phrase does not make the answer to a question about nine a translation or a same-quantity counterpart.

These are numeric rule comparisons, not newly supplied Russian phrases. In other cases the numeral and noun need the relevant declined forms.

### PROVENANCE `prov-ca-u22-declension-rem-body`

- Objectives: obj-u22-declension
- Sessions: u22-s-orient, u22-s-declension
- Source: src-content-audit-research
- Rights: rights-content-audit-research
- Classification: none
- Current review status: IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT
- Content hash: 134ee10fb7eb3ee82779d144a406adce3ffeec15638cee9d6c8446fe54bd2756

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-declension:declension-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
двум — Дательный, the dative. двумя — Творительный, the instrumental. Both are declined forms of the numeral meaning two. Dative commonly marks a recipient or goal; instrumental can mark accompaniment or means. The final part of the form distinguishes these two shown examples; it is not enough to recognize the quantity two.

Дательный means dative; Творительный means instrumental; Предложный means prepositional. A form must be interpreted with its construction. The bare form двух can occur in more than one case. The admitted row includes the preposition and is labelled prepositional; the check does not claim that a bare ending always uniquely identifies a case. In oblique number phrases the plural noun normally carries the case required by the construction.

(о) двух / (об) обоих / (о) полутора — Предложный. This complete row gives the prepositional forms for two, both and one and a half. The parentheses show the accompanying preposition; it is not part of the numeral itself. The prepositional choice contrasts with the dative двум and instrumental двумя. Recognizing this row does not demonstrate independently declining a complete number phrase.
shippedText=двум — Дательный, the dative. двумя — Творительный, the instrumental. Both are declined forms of the numeral meaning two. Dative commonly marks a recipient or goal; instrumental can mark accompaniment or means. The final part of the form distinguishes these two shown examples; it is not enough to recognize the quantity two.

Дательный means dative; Творительный means instrumental; Предложный means prepositional. A form must be interpreted with its construction. The bare form двух can occur in more than one case. The admitted row includes the preposition and is labelled prepositional; the check does not claim that a bare ending always uniquely identifies a case. In oblique number phrases the plural noun normally carries the case required by the construction.

(о) двух / (об) обоих / (о) полутора — Предложный. This complete row gives the prepositional forms for two, both and one and a half. The parentheses show the accompanying preposition; it is not part of the numeral itself. The prepositional choice contrasts with the dative двум and instrumental двумя. Recognizing this row does not demonstrate independently declining a complete number phrase.

sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-half-forms:half-forms-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
полтора́ рубля́ — 1.5 roubles. полторы́ мину́ты — 1.5 minutes. The first numeral form is masculine/neuter; the second is feminine. In the nominative and accusative construction, one and a half takes a genitive singular noun. The noun determines the numeral gender even though the amount exceeds one. The retained alternative gloss one and a half hours / an hour and a half names hours, not roubles or minutes.

In the other cases, the numeral has the shared form полутора, and the noun is plural in the case required by the construction. The numeral form alone therefore cannot tell you whether a phrase is genitive, dative or instrumental. Compare the preposition and the noun ending. This differs from the genitive singular noun after the nominative/accusative forms.

из полу́тора рубле́й — from 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires genitive plural on the noun. к полу́тора рубля́м — to 1.5 roubles: the preposition requires dative plural. The English distractor with 1.5 roubles names accompaniment/means with an instrumental noun in the corresponding retained source construction. The amount stays one and a half while the preposition changes the relationship. The check asks for the meaning of the whole shown phrase, including from or to.

In the other cases, the numeral has the shared form полутора, and the noun is plural in the case required by the construction. The numeral form alone therefore cannot tell you whether a phrase is genitive, dative or instrumental. Compare the preposition and the noun ending. This differs from the genitive singular noun after the nominative/accusative forms.

- Content hash: 5eb7f78178919ca3c08f3bc9f4a56557919bee96590c656e6e824a0553457b20

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u22-noun-forms:noun-forms-rem-body
originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
один мальчик — one boy. одна девушка — one girl. The numeral meaning one agrees with its noun in gender, number and case. These displayed counting phrases use a singular noun: the masculine boy takes the masculine numeral, and the feminine girl takes the feminine numeral. The guided choice asks for another phrase with one, not the same noun.

For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.

две девушки — two girls. три кошки — three cats. Both displayed nouns are genitive singular after these numerals, although the quantity is greater than one and these endings can resemble nominative plural. The numeral for two has a feminine form; the form for three does not change for the noun gender. The check asks for another example in the two-through-four group, not an English translation of the cue.

пять девушек — five girls. десять копеек — ten kopeks. Here the nouns are genitive plural. Five and ten belong to the same noun-form group; one and two do not. Numbers whose last two digits are 11 through 14 also use this group, including 111 through 114. These choices recognize supplied number phrases; they do not test independently producing every noun ending.
shippedText=один мальчик — one boy. одна девушка — one girl. The numeral meaning one agrees with its noun in gender, number and case. These displayed counting phrases use a singular noun: the masculine boy takes the masculine numeral, and the feminine girl takes the feminine numeral. The guided choice asks for another phrase with one, not the same noun.

For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.

две девушки — two girls. три кошки — three cats. Both displayed nouns are genitive singular after these numerals, although the quantity is greater than one and these endings can resemble nominative plural. The numeral for two has a feminine form; the form for three does not change for the noun gender. The check asks for another example in the two-through-four group, not an English translation of the cue.

пять девушек — five girls. десять копеек — ten kopeks. Here the nouns are genitive plural. Five and ten belong to the same noun-form group; one and two do not. Numbers whose last two digits are 11 through 14 also use this group, including 111 through 114. These choices recognize supplied number phrases; they do not test independently producing every noun ending.

один мальчик — one boy. одна девушка — one girl. The numeral meaning one agrees with its noun in gender, number and case. These displayed counting phrases use a singular noun: the masculine boy takes the masculine numeral, and the feminine girl takes the feminine numeral. The guided choice asks for another phrase with one, not the same noun.

originalSourceText=Retained licensed numeral spans; Cornell Beginning Russian Grammar gr11_d_1.htm, le51_56_b_num.htm, gr11_d_3.htm; Gramota Pismovnik numeral usage. Consulted 2026-10-11; independently authored English; review pending.
For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.
shippedText=For nominative counting phrases, one takes a singular noun, two through four take a genitive singular noun, and five through twenty take a genitive plural noun. Apply this shortcut to an accusative phrase only where its forms coincide with the nominative. Animate accusative phrases and other cases need their own case forms; the shortcut is not a rule that all number phrases keep these endings.

две девушки — two girls. три кошки — three cats. Both displayed nouns are genitive singular after these numerals, although the quantity is greater than one and these endings can resemble nominative plural. The numeral for two has a feminine form; the form for three does not change for the noun gender. The check asks for another example in the two-through-four group, not an English translation of the cue.

пять девушек — five girls. десять копеек — ten kopeks. Here the nouns are genitive plural. Five and ten belong to the same noun-form group; one and two do not. Numbers whose last two digits are 11 through 14 also use this group, including 111 through 114. These choices recognize supplied number phrases; they do not test independently producing every noun ending.

- Content hash: 1d0c1714a27d8da10d07a62aaabb357ffec80257d8323f39e1d35d47dd2446a8

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collectiv
originalSourceText=several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collective, and also fractional constructions; also it has other types of words, relative to numbers: collective adverbial forms (вдвоём), multiplicative (двойной) and counting-system (двоичный) adjectives, some numeric-pronominal and indefinite quantity words (сколько, много, несколько). Here are the numerals from 0 to 10:

{| class="wikitable"
!
! cardinal numbers
! ordinal numbers<br />(nominative case, masculine)
! collective numbers
|-
! 0
| ноль ''or'' нуль || нулево́й || rowspan="2" | —
|-
! 1
| оди́н ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}), одна́ ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}), одно́ ({{abbr|n.|neuter}}), одни́ ({{abbr|pl.|plural}})<br />(раз may be used when counting, a colloquial option) || пе́рвый 
|-
! 2
| два ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}, {{abbr|n.|neuter}}), две ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}) || второ́й || дво́е
|-
! 3
| три || тре́тий || тро́е
|-
! 4
| четы́ре || четвёртый || че́тверо
|-
! 5
| пять || пя́тый || пя́теро
|-
! 6
| шесть || шесто́й || ше́стеро
|-
! 7
| семь || седьмо́й || се́меро
|-
! 8
| во́семь || восьмо́й || (во́сьмеро)<ref>Collective numerals for more than 7 are seldom used.</ref>
|-
! 9
| де́вять || девя́тый || (де́вятеро)
|-
! 10
| де́сять || деся́тый || (де́сятеро)
|}
shippedText=дво́е
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/

### PROVENANCE `prov-ex-u22-collective-0-ru`

- Source: src-wikipedia-russian-grammar-1369617627

- Content hash: da4e6dd54ef9de6fd6dc5ad78a275970366c277c0e78b231ae299bf986583f92

recordType=example
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collectiv
originalSourceText=several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collective, and also fractional constructions; also it has other types of words, relative to numbers: collective adverbial forms (вдвоём), multiplicative (двойной) and counting-system (двоичный) adjectives, some numeric-pronominal and indefinite quantity words (сколько, много, несколько). Here are the numerals from 0 to 10:

{| class="wikitable"
!
! cardinal numbers
! ordinal numbers<br />(nominative case, masculine)
! collective numbers
|-
! 0
| ноль ''or'' нуль || нулево́й || rowspan="2" | —
|-
! 1
| оди́н ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}), одна́ ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}), одно́ ({{abbr|n.|neuter}}), одни́ ({{abbr|pl.|plural}})<br />(раз may be used when counting, a colloquial option) || пе́рвый 
|-
! 2
| два ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}, {{abbr|n.|neuter}}), две ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}) || второ́й || дво́е
|-
! 3
| три || тре́тий || тро́е
|-
! 4
| четы́ре || четвёртый || че́тверо
|-
! 5
| пять || пя́тый || пя́теро
|-
! 6
| шесть || шесто́й || ше́стеро
|-
! 7
| семь || седьмо́й || се́меро
|-
! 8
| во́семь || восьмо́й || (во́сьмеро)<ref>Collective numerals for more than 7 are seldom used.</ref>
|-
! 9
| де́вять || девя́тый || (де́вятеро)
|-
! 10
| де́сять || деся́тый || (де́сятеро)
|}
shippedText=два
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikipedia contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikipedia:Copyrights oldid 1369466282. Page history for oldid 1369617627 identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.
sourceHash=5da558630e9ee6f8d46e13b4a8485e3cc595f337b419ecec32a50085ebc99b8a

sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think 
originalSourceText=Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).

originalSourceText=Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).

#: {{uxi|ru|'''полтора́''' [[рубль|рубля́]]|1.5 roubles}}
#: {{uxi|ru|[[полторы́]] [[минута|мину́ты]]|1.5 minutes}}
#: {{uxi|ru|[[из]] '''полу́тора''' [[рубль|рубле́й]]|from 1.5 roubles}}

#: {{uxi|ru|'''полтора́''' [[час|часа́]]|one and a half hours / an hour and a half}}

====Usage notes====
In the nominative and accusative, this number governs the [[genitive case|genitive]] '''singular''' of the noun. In all of the other cases, it governs the '''plural''' of the noun in the same case as the number.

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Numbers?oldid=4371358
sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, 
originalSourceText=The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).
shippedText=one boy
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).

sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, 
originalSourceText=The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).

 | ''двумя'' / ''обеими'' / ''полутора''
 |-
 ! colspan="2"  | Предложный  
 | ''(о) двух'' / ''(об) обоих'' / ''(о) полутора''
 | ''(о) двух'' / ''(об) обеих'' / ''(о) полутора''
 |}

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collectiv
originalSourceText=several classes of numerals ([имена] числительные): cardinal, ordinal, collective, and also fractional constructions; also it has other types of words, relative to numbers: collective adverbial forms (вдвоём), multiplicative (двойной) and counting-system (двоичный) adjectives, some numeric-pronominal and indefinite quantity words (сколько, много, несколько). Here are the numerals from 0 to 10:

{| class="wikitable"
!
! cardinal numbers
! ordinal numbers<br />(nominative case, masculine)
! collective numbers
|-
! 0
| ноль ''or'' нуль || нулево́й || rowspan="2" | —
|-
! 1
| оди́н ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}), одна́ ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}), одно́ ({{abbr|n.|neuter}}), одни́ ({{abbr|pl.|plural}})<br />(раз may be used when counting, a colloquial option) || пе́рвый 
|-
! 2
| два ({{abbr|m.|masculine}}, {{abbr|n.|neuter}}), две ({{abbr|f.|feminine}}) || второ́й || дво́е
|-
! 3
| три || тре́тий || тро́е
|-
! 4
| четы́ре || четвёртый || че́тверо
|-
! 5
| пять || пя́тый || пя́теро
|-
! 6
| шесть || шесто́й || ше́стеро
|-
! 7
| семь || седьмо́й || се́меро
|-
! 8
| во́семь || восьмо́й || (во́сьмеро)<ref>Collective numerals for more than 7 are seldom used.</ref>
|-
! 9
| де́вять || девя́тый || (де́вятеро)
|-
! 10
| де́сять || деся́тый || (де́сятеро)
|}
shippedText=collective numbers
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikipedia contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikipedia:Copyrights oldid 1369466282. Page history for oldid 1369617627 identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.
sourceHash=5da558630e9ee6f8d46e13b4a8485e3cc595f337b419ecec32a50085ebc99b8a
contentHash=f8156090998accef456c2f179558dd3bc6acac87f2a2f50e4d2d6b043be27474

sourceRecordId=numbers:Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think 
originalSourceText=Numerals larger than twenty follow the above rules using the last digit.  Think of numerals ending in zero as ending in ten.  For example:  тридцать три рюмки (thirty-three shot glasses), сорок один год (forty-one years), шестьдесят девять слов (sixty-nine words).

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Russian/Numbers?oldid=4371358
sourceRevision=4371358
sourceRecordId=numbers:The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, 
originalSourceText=The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).
shippedText=The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Wikibooks contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Page history for each frozen oldid identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

originalSourceText=The numeral один (1) should be used in the proper form to indicate gender (and, number, believe it or not).  For example: один мальчик (one boy), одна девушка (one girl), одно животное (one animal), одни джинсы (one [pair of] jeans).  The plural form of the numeral (1) is a bit of an oddity, but its use will become clear with time.  Following a form of один should come the item at hand in the nominative or inanimate accusative case, as appropriate.

The numerals два (or две), три, and четыре all require the following item to be presented in the genitive singular form.  The numeral два has two forms: два for masculine and neuter items and две for feminine items.  After the numeral два, each numeral has only a single form.  See for example:  две девушки (two girls), три кошки (three cats), четыре мальчика (four boys).  

The numerals пять (5) through двадцать (20) require their attached items in the genitive plural forms, as in these examples:  пять девушек (five girls), десять копеек (ten kopeks), шестнадцать рублей (sixteen rubles).
